@author: Perseonn Balthasaar
@updated: 2020-12-10

This guide is intended to be a comprehensive version of the infographics-like Summoner Guide I have made before. This expands the idea of sub classes, pet attacks, and equipment. Updates will be done regularly based on new material or discovery. Name will follow the official North American translation, and will also provide the Japanese name and the fan-translation name.

[[include comp:pso2-nav]]

[!-- ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== Su Basic Part ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== --]

+ Basics Summoner
[[image info-1-su-basics-1.avif | Summoner Basics]]
A summoner is a class that utilizes [[span class="icon pet"]]pets[[/span]] to inflict damage at enemies. The pets are basically the summoner’s weapon, with their own stats and Photon Arts (PA). Pets are most damaging when using Summoner main as the class have a couple of ways to raise the pet damage. Being a Summoner is relatively more expensive compared to other classes: instead of eating up your assets, it will directly eat your Meseta, time, and your precious SG.

++ Starting as Summoner
[[image info-1-su-basics-2.avif | Getting Started]]
If you're new, you should follow the ARKS Mission since it will lead you to these 3 important things: Meeting Pietro, Meeting Ariel, and doing the Summoner Tutorial Quest
+++ Meeting Pietro
Pietro is located on the Gate Area; he is mentor for Summoner Class. Activities you can do with Pietro includes:
* Request Limit Break, for rarity ★12 and above.
 * Pet needs to be L100 to utilize Limit Break
 * Allows ★12 pet to attain L110, ★13 to L120, ★14 to L130
 * Pet will grow //depressingly slow// above L100

+++ Meeting Ariel
Ariel is in in the Shop Area, next to Monica / Dudu; she facilitates various pet enhancement activities. Activities that you can do with Ariel includes:
||~ Pet Lab ||~ Description ||

|| Pet Goods Shop || [[span class="cell-item"]]Exchange various items to obtain candies and tools.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]You can exchange for [[span class="icon item-tool"]]Grinders[[/span]] for [[span class="icon item-tool"]]Sweets Dissolver (キャンディーイレイサー Candy Eraser)[[/span]] and [[span class="icon item-tool"]]Excubes[[/span]] for  [[span class="icon item-tool"]]Caramel Crusher (キャラメルクラッシャー)[[/span]].[[/span]] ||
|| Evolve Pet Rarity || [[span class="cell-item"]]Increases pet rarity with another egg of same or higher rarity.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Also used to change pet's personality.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]More info on pet's rarity below.[[/span]] ||
|| Return Pet to Egg || [[span class="cell-item"]]Destroy their entire sweets box.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Returned pet egg will be untradable.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]If you hatch a returned egg, it will respawn all the Caramel Cubes and have zeroed feed count[[/span]] ||


+++ Summoner Tutorial Quest
Check the Tutorial Quests on Quest Counter to know the basic way on how Summoner fights.


++ Summoner Mag
[[image info-1-mag-1.avif | Mag Basics]]
At a certain point early in the game, you will get a Mag. If you are to use Summoner as-is. You will want a 200 [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC/T-ATK[[/span]] Mag (unless you plan to sub Bouncer or Phantom, see below). Only increase the [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC[[/span]] on your mag, and nothing else.

Although Summoner can use [[span class="icon stat-satk"]]MEL/SATK[[/span]] or [[span class="icon stat-ratk"]]RNG/R-ATK[[/span]] for pets, focusing on [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC[[/span]] is easier for the endgame:
* [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC[[/span]] affixes are relatively cheaper than the other two.
* Mag will have PP Restore J action which boosts PP regeneration at certain interval. While unreliable, it's helpful when active.
* Resta, or healing depends on [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC[[/span]] only.
* Rappy Melody's heals depends on their and character's [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC[[/span]].
* Rykros Staff's [[span class="icon op-pot"]]Sinister Stare (Glance of Darkness)[[/span]] depends on character's [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC[[/span]].

[[include comp:pso2-manaka
|content=It's easier to just use TEC/T-ATK Mag! Just fill your Mag with tech weapon like Staff, Wand, Talis, or Jet Boots, and reduce its DEX by feeding it with Mates or ★6 Room Goods!]]

[[include comp:pso2-manaka
|content=MEL/S-ATK Mag is not recommended! It will only support Fi's Crazy Beat and will be obsolete once you use Phantom sub! Don't make a special mag just to support 1 skill! For a bit more info please see [#mel-v-tec-mag the FAQ!] ]]

++ Fixing Mag
[[image info-1-mag-2.avif | Fixing Mag]]

If you messed up your mag's stat, you can fix them by using a [[span class="icon item-devc"]]Mag Level Down Device[[/span]], which costs 5 [[span class="icon item-tool"]]Excubes[[/span]]. [[span class="icon item-devc"]]Excubes[[/span]] are rare commodities until you can do XH+ Quests which requires level 80+.


++ Getting Eggs via Collection Files (CF)
[[image info-1-cf.avif | Collection File Eggs]]

You can get decent eggs from Collection Files (CF) that Ms. Pudding provides. CF is basically killing specified target mobs until you gain a required item; once you obtained all required items, you will automatically get the reward item. For example, to get a ★13 Jinga from the Summoner Support CF, you need to obtain 6 items from killing any enemy, Mechs, and Blu Ringahda. This is very easy to do, particularly because all enemies required for the items can be found on the Quarry.

CF has few quirks including:
* CF required item increase rate can be boosted by using Rare Drop Rate, Tri Boost, and anything that boosts rare drop
* CF required item gauge will only increase if you clear the quest. If you kill enemies and abandon quest, the CF will not raise.
* On normal circumstance, a CF required item will only drop if you start a quest at 100% gauge. Simply put, you need to have at least 2 runs for a CF item to drop: 1 run to max it to 100%, the other is to drop the items. If you start the quest with the gauge at 99.99%, the item will not drop and clearing the quest will simply put the gauge to 100%.

[[include comp:pso2-manaka
|content=Wanda-Jinga has no particular weakness and is easy to use! If you see them on CF, grab them first!]]

++ Summoner Skill Tree
[[image info-2-su-skill-tree.avif | Summoner Skill Tree]]

+++ Recommended Skill Tree
You can follow this skill progression that's guaranteed to get all of the essential skills for Summoner main. This applies to both PSO2 NA and PSO2 Jp.
||~ Level ||~ Skills ||

|| [[span class="cell-item"]]6 to 10[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]All Attack Bonus 1 (3 → 5)[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Pet Sympathy (0 → 3)[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Sidestep Jump[[/span]] || 
|| [[span class="cell-item"]]11 to 15[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]Harmonize Up (0 → 1)[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Easy Sympathy (0 → 1)[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Long Distance Love (0 → 1)[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]All Attack Bonus 2 (0 → 2)[[/span]] || 
|| [[span class="cell-item"]]16 to 20[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]All Attack Bonus 2 (2 → 5)[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Summoner's Mark / Point Assist (0 → 2)[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]First Arts Perfect/JA Addition[[/span]] || 
|| [[span class="cell-item"]]21 to 25[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]Summoner's Mark / Point Assist (2 → 5)[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Enhanced Mark / Support Fire (0 → 1)[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Assist Share (0 → 1)[[/span]] ||

+++ Dia Master (Dear Master) Exploit
Exploiting the mechanic of Dear Master, can grant you //infinite HP//. I personally think this gives Summoner unfair advantage in "high difficulty quest" (which enemy just do excessive damage), but to each their own. Dear Master, as noted, transfers the damage to pet and gives you invulnerability, **essentially adding pet's HP to yours**. This redirected damage can be **mitigated**:
* Triggering Maron/Melon's Fierce personality that recovers HP (by extension, also their Cute personality)
* Triggering Jinga's Fervent personality (by extension, also their Powerful personality)
This will allow you to counter and subdue aggressive bosses like Phaleg or Omega Masquerade easily, especially since healing to pet is rarely nerfed.

Contrary of what I've written before, the damage transferred to pet is:
* Not subject to pet defense or damage cuts and is applied as-is. You can try this by seeing that Melon mostly take 1 damage from EP4 Phaleg, and when Dear Master triggers, they take hundreds of damage.
* Not subject to guard frames or invulnerability frames, e.g. it will still hit Shinkuro during Kick / Kyaku and Viola during Change.
I apologize for the error and the misconception I've caused.

[[image dear-master-melon-1.avif | Melon takes 1 damage from Phaleg's stun attack.]]
[[image dear-master-melon-2.avif | Melon takes 319 is the transferred damage as well as 1 damage since it also get hit.]]
[[image dear-master-viola-change.avif | Viola takes 483 damage during Viola Change, which is an invulnerable state]]
[[image dear-master-synchro-kyaku.avif | Synchro takes 513 damage during Shinkuro Kick, which is a guard frame state]]



++ Pet Raising
[[image info-3-pet-raising.avif | Pet Raising]]

Your pet has a different mechanics that that of a normal weaponry. Here, pet raising will be discussed from the basic topics like its parameters, to the more advanced topics, like its candy box.

+++ Pet's Properties
Pet has several important properties, from Rarity, Personality, Elemental Attribute, and Pet Plus.

[[image info-s-egg.avif | A window displaying egg information.]]


++++ Rarity and Rarity Upgrade
[[image info-3-pet-evolution.avif | Pet Evolution]]

A pet has a rarity that determines their highest level. Rarity can be upgraded at the Pet Lab if you have a higher rarity egg of the same species (i.e. You can only increase rarity of a ★7 Viola with a ★8 Viola). Once a pet reaches L100, you can Limit Break them by asking Pietro for free.
||~ Rarity ||~ Max Level ||~ Limit Break||
|| ★6 || L60 || No ||
|| ★7 || L65 || No ||
|| ★8 || L70 || No ||
|| ★9 || L75 || No ||
|| ★10 || L85 || No ||
|| ★11 || L90 || No ||
|| ★12 || L110 || Yes ||
|| ★13 || L120 || Yes ||
|| ★14 || L130 || Yes ||

Rarity upgrade also allows you to change pet's personality, provided the fodder egg has different personality and at least of the same rarity.

Wanda, Trim, and Sally can be rarity upgraded to Jinga, Aero, and Popple, respectively. Be warned that if you say, evolve Wanda to Jinga, you need another Jinga Egg to rarity upgrade it. There are no reason to pick Wanda over Jinga, Trim over Aero, or Sally over Popple. Jinga, Aero, and Popple are superior in every aspects, unless your Wanda, Torim, and Cery are ★14.

[[include comp:pso2-manaka
|content=Rarity upgrade and pet evolution can only be done on Ariel's Item Lab! Don't feed that expensive high evolution egg to your main pet, it will only raise it's EXP!]]

++++ Personality
Personality is similar to weapon potential. They have varied effect, but most prominent is increasing damage. Personality has 5 levels and is automatically raised as pet levels up (Pet L100 is where you can attain L5 Personality). Pets up to ★13 rarity have a preferred personality, as shown below; but this becomes irrelevant once pet reaches ★14 rarity, which gives it a third personality that combines both personality. Changing personality can be hard or impossible as it requires egg of at least the same rarity.

||~ Icon ||~ Pet ||~ Preferred ★13 Personality ||~ ★14 Personality ||

|| [[image /img/pso2/item/item-egg-vulcan.png | | pso2-icon double]] || [[span class="cell-item icon pet"]]Vulcan[[/span]] ||  || [[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]アグレッシブ[[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]Aggressive[[/span]] ||


"Very/Total" (超 //Super//)" personality (e.g. Very Thoughtful) will have an additional effect of raising Rare Drop Rate by 5%. "Very" personality's effect is included on a pet's ★14 personality. Please note that "Very" personality is not worth the price. If you can get one for free, feel free use it (or sell it), else don't bother with it.

[[include comp:pso2-manaka
|content=Ensure you have the personality you want! Changing personality requires rarity upgrade, which needs same or higher rarity egg!]]


++++ Elemental Attribute
Pets starts with a 10 attribute value that is visible upon viewing the egg. Ideally, you will want the default attribute to be Light. You can change attribute by putting Lemonade in the Candy Box, but changing to Light Attribute is very costly. Attribute can be raised by putting Pancakes. Pets with ★12 rarity or below will have a maximum of 50 attribute, while a ★13 or above will have 60 attribute.

Attribute directly affects pet damage. Let's say you have a pet with 1000 Pwr (any power type) with 50 Light attribute. While in the information screen the pet has 1000 ATK, it effectively has:

> 1000 Pwr + 50/100 * 1000 Pwr = 1500 Pwr

If the pet attacks a target which is weak to Light element, its effective attack will be:

> 1000 Pwr + 50/100 * 1.2 * 1000 Pwr = 1600 Pwr

And thus, raising element is the best way to increase overall pet's damage.

[[include comp:pso2-manaka
|content=Always hatch a Light-attribute egg, since almost all raid bosses are weak to Light!]]


++++ Level / Egg Synthesis
Level up pet by using Egg Synthesis on Pet menu. L1 pet is only as frightening as a Rappy Doll room item. A pet can be fed (synthesized) with an EXP Egg or another pet egg. If you are a Summoner main, you will have a steady drop of EXP Eggs. The Pet Lab can exchange grinders for ★6 EXP egg or Photon Sphere for ★12 EXP Egg.

[[include comp:pso2-manaka
|content=Use Synthesis Charge to boost the EXP gained from eggs! However, it will cost 10 SG each for the first 3 times, and also use high-rarity eggs only, like ★12 EXP Egg.]]


++++ Pet Plus
A pet has extra HP, [[span class="icon triple stat-aatk"]]MEL-RNG-TEC/S-R-T ATK[[/span]] and [[span class="icon stat-dex"]]DEX[[/span]] to be raised. This is called Pet Plus, as seen on the figure below.

[[image info-s-pet-plus.avif | A Pet Plus of a Rappy, not yet maximized.]]

Each pet type has a different plus value cap; any Wanda will have same the same cap, but a Jinga will have different cap than Wanda. Pet Plus can make or break pet: Rappy is quite weak without its all stat +250 Pet Plus cap. Plus value will usually be close-to-maxed, if not maxed, once they reach L130 by feeding random Pet eggs.

[[image info-s-egg-synthesis.avif | Feeding another pet egg to increase the Pet Plus.]]


++++ Power Synthesis
If you want to quickly raise your Pet Plus value, you can use this method:
* Hatch a low rarity egg you don't use, lets call this fodder pet.
* Feed the fodder pet with another egg to increase it's pet plus value.
* Return the fodder pet back to egg.
* Feed the fodder egg to your main pet and gain much more pet plus with the cost of use 1 synthesis slot!

[[image info-3-pet-power-synthesis.avif | Gain a lot of pet plus!]]


++ Sweets Box (Candy Box)
S1 Box is akin to putting affix to regular weapons or units. A pet always starts with a 8x8 Sweets Box that is filled with Gelatin Cube (Blue), Caramel Cube (Orange), and Paper Cube (Gray). Each pet type has a different box layout, for the exception of Wanda-Jinga, Trim-Aero, and Sally-Popple, Redran-Herbie, and Shinkuro-Vulcan

Below is an example of a fresh Wanda-Jinga box, note that it is filled with several cubes:
* Blue Gelatin Cubes will be removed automatically once a pet reaches level 50 (or so).
* Brown Caramel Cubes, require [[span class="icon item-pass"]]Caramel Crusher[[/span]] (8 [[span class="icon item-tool"]]Excubes[[/span]]) to remove. 
 * A pet will always start with 8 Caramel Cubes, requiring you to spend 64 [[span class="icon item-tool"]]Excubes[[/span]] per pet.
* Gray Paper Cube cannot be removed. Makes you wonder why since they are supposed to be paper.

[[image box-wanda-blank.avif | Freshly hatched Wanda's Sweets Box]]



+++ Sweets Types
A Sweets Box can hold a limited amount of candies. It is a good idea to compress candies first before putting it to Sweets Box!

[[/div]]

++ Summoner's Progression
[[image info-1-su-progression.avif | Summoner Progression]]


Your main progress as a Summoner should be as follow:
* Have a Light-attributed Jinga fully raised, i.e. full sweets/candy box, and at L130
* Have a Light-attributed Melon fully raised
* Have a Light-attributed Synchro fully raised
* Have a [[span class="icon wp-ta"]]Rykros Staff +30[[/span]], easiest to obtain from trading 500 Conqueror Crest at Zieg's Conqueror Crest Exhcange
 * You can accomplish this by trading 63 Sage Crest into 504 Conqueror Crest, also at Zieg's Sage Crest Exchange
* Have 3 ★13 unit with 150 [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC/T-ATK[[/span]] and 25 PP each (unit's base stat included!)
 * In the example above, the base unit is a Schvelle which has 50[[span class="icon triple stat-aatk"]]ATK[[/span]] and 12 PP by default. The ability affix adds 120 [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC[[/span]] and 10 PP, making it has a total of 170 [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC[[/span]] and 22 PP. This is less than the ideal 25 PP, but it can still work.
* Have 200 PP and become SuPh, the best class combo available for Summoner.
 * In normal circumstance, especially for beginners, you start with 100 PP.
 * L90 Summoner can get 5 PP from PP Up skill.
 * Ph sub gives additional 20 PP from PP High Up skill.
 * This totals to 125 PP base, so you need 75 from equipment, which is why your 3 units ideally need to have 25 PP each to attain 200 PP total.
 * There are other ways to gain PP, such as putting Spirita Cookie to your Pet's Candy Box
 * You don't need 200 PP if you aren't using Ph sub.

+++ Tips for Beginner ~ Don't make a Common Mistake!

|content=If someone recommends you a MEL/S-ATK Mag for Summoner, most likely they are using old knowledge from 2017s! Use TEC/T-ATK Mag instead!]]
[[include comp:pso2-manaka
|content=Did someone recommend you Marron as best pet? That's also an old knowledge from 2017! Marron have a limited use outside of some bosses!]]

|content=Please level up your pet! Without a proper level, they don't do much damage and dies easily!]]

+ Intermediate Summoner
[[include comp:pso2-manaka
|content=Hi, are you familiar yet with pet raising and sweets box? If not, please read it on the basics!]]

++ Class Combination
[[image info-2-su-subclass.avif  | Summoner Subclass]]

Summoner has 4 viable subclasses: the common Fighter and Phantom, and the more niche Braver and Bouncer:
* SuFi: Most common combination. Best combination if Ph is not yet available
* SuPh: The premier combo for everything. It is stronger than SuFi with great PP regeneration, but requires the character to have 200 PP
* SuBr: Great for beginners, especially players new to Summoner **and** new to PSO2
* SuBo: Has good PP regen and strong against breakable parts, but difficult to use.

All multipliers listed are not counting High Level Bonus, which is a must-take. The "Damage Rating" stated is based on critical builds, whose explanation can be found after this section.


+++ Summoner-Fighter (SuFi)
Recommended for all skill levels. SuFi has the highest stance multiplier and less likely need to switch stance, but offers little compensation once PP runs out and has lowest critical rate (means your base damage will be lower). Despite this, SuFi remains well-rounded and is a good choice towards end-game, especially if Phantom is not available.

||~ Property ||~ Description ||
|| Damage rating || 174.09% (Brave Stance), 198.40% (Wise Stance)^^[[link #sub-damage-calculation | Explanation]]^^ ||
|| PP management || Normal ||
|| Ease of use || Moderate ||
|| Usage note || Abusing Dear Master on overly-aggressive bosses allows you to activate Slayer skills perpetually. ||

++++ Fighter Skills to Take
||~ Icon ||~ Skills ||~ SP ||~ Description ||

+++ Summoner-Phantom (SuPh)
The final destination of Summoner. SuPh has high multiplier, high PP regeneration, and no stances, making it easy to use. In every way, compared to other subs, Ph stands superior as an all rounder. However, it requires 200 PP to be effective, 2 level 75 classes to unlock Ph, and Ph weaponry to sustain its leveling since Ph cannot have a subclass.

|| Usage note || Requires 200 PP. The only sub class that allows easier use of Redran. ||

+++ Summoner-Luster (SuLu)
An alternative to SuPh, mainly if you are confident in your ability to keep attacking your target. Luster sub heavily relies on the combo count, or voltage, to do proper damage since the more combo you do, the more critical rate you have. You'll need both Shinkuro and Zanverse to keep your damage up.

|| PP management || Normal ||
|| Ease of use || Hard ||
|| Usage note || Requires reaching 500 Voltage as fast as possible; Activating Alter Ego during 500 Voltage will make Alter Ego immediately usable after the effect ends. ||

+++ Summoner-Braver (SuBr)
Recommended for beginners. SuBr has the easiest usage, but relatively lacks output. This is compensated by Attack Advance which, on several pets, provides a PA-powered normal attacks with 0 PP usage. This contributes to its beginner-friendly nature as newbies tend to use normal attacks a lot. SuBr is best when fighting bosses whose weak point is exposed for a long time to warrant Weak Stance activation, such as Dark Falz Elder, Cuento Dragon, Dark Falz Persona, Demoire Dominus, and Veiled Fordruss.

||~ Property ||~ Description ||
|| Damage rating || 154.06% (Average Stance with Attack Advance), 180.86% (Weak Stance with Attack Advance)^^[[link #sub-damage-calculation | Explanation]]^^ ||
|| PP management || Normal ||
|| Ease of use || Easy ||
|| Best compatibility || Rappy. If your Rappy has Spirited Parfait (はりきりパフェ), you can just use normal attacks since it will become Rappy's top DPS move, being only slightly (~5%) less powerful than Rappy End with S Charge. ||
|| Usage note || With a strong normal attack pets, Near Auto Target, Attack Advance, and Spirited Parfait, you can just wave your takt once and cruise through any fields without using PAs. ||

+++ Summoner-Bouncer (SuBo)
SuBo has a superior PP regeneration mechanism. They are the only sub-class that allows Summonner to reach 100% critical without external help (means your base damage is all-time highest). While it does not have high multiplier compared to Fi, its PP regeneration is more than enough to make it up, even with only Elemental Stance; this means that you will need to get used to PA constantly. Unfortunately, using SuBo means you require extra palette slot to accommodate Fields, and you will be required to maintain your Shifta and Deband. SuBo is best when fighting bosses whose break point is exposed for a long time to warrant Break Stance activation, such as Dark/Omega Falz Luther, Profound/Unfathomable Darkness, Magatsu, Quent Dragon, and Dark Falz Persona.

|| Ease of use || Hard ||
|| Best compatibility || Synchro. Use Rykros Staff for maximum effectiveness. ||
|| Usage note || If you need to create elemental pets to take advantage of Elemental Stance, Wanda or Jinga would be the best choice. If you don't have elemental Pets, SuBo is less effective on multi-elemental weakness EQs, such as the Seasonal EQs. ||

+++ Other Class Combinations
Other than Fi, Ph, Br, and Bo, no other class combination works for Su:
||~ Sub ||~ Multiplier with Critical Ring ||
|| Hunter || 115.71% ||
|| Force || 110.51% ||
|| Force (Photon Flare) || 120.92% ||
|| Techer (Elemental Weak) || 137.61% ||
|| Etoile || 143.67% ||
|| Etoile (Tech Attack Count Bonus) ||165.22% ||
* Su doesn't have JA, which means SuHu and SuEt will not work well:
 * SuHu gains nothing from JA Bonus; Fury Stance only boost Striking and Ranged while all Pet attacks are considered Tech (regardless of Switch skills)
 * SuEt cannot activate Flat Attack bonus because this skill only applies to missing JA on attacks that have JA. Pet attacks have no JA
 * SuEt doesn't gain any critical rate from Etoile tree, making its base damage lower.
 * SuEt with Tech Attack Count Bonus is incompatible with a lot of pets' combo which involve using a single attack repeatedly
* Pet attacks, while always do Tech damage, is not a Technic:
 * Elemental Mastery (e.g. Fire Mastery, Light Mastery, etc.) skills are invalid due to only working for Technics
 * Similarly, SuFo cannot activate any Tech Advance skills (Normal Tech Advance, Tech Charge Advance, Tech JA Advance)


[[include comp:pso2-manaka
|content=Using SuFo and SuTe for casting attack Tech along with pet is a bad idea! You'll end up with weak tech and weak pet!]]


++ About Critical Hits
While Su does not have JA, this is offset by pets having 70% minimum damage, as opposed to other weapon's 90% when using a ★13. Adding as much critical as possible allows pet to reach higher average damage, without compensating any aspects (maybe that 10 PP from 2 Spirit Canes).

[[include comp:pso2-manaka
|content=Stack as many critical rate up as you can, it's a shortcut to high damage!]]

+++ Simplified Calculation
By default, pet's minimum damage is roughly 70%, while Critical hits in PSO2 automatically put your damage to 100%. Thus, without any enhancements, your mean damage using a pet is:

> (100% + 70%) / 2 = 85%

However, at any circumstances, you have at least 5% critical rate, which means 95% of time you deal average 85% damage, while the 5% of the time, you deal 100% of the damage.

> 95% * 85% + 5% * 100% = 85.75%

In other words, without any critical enhancements, averagely, you only do 85.75% damage.

Lets say you are using a Class Stance Critical (25%, assuming either Br or Bo sub) and a Critical Strike Ring Tech (20%), you will have a total of 50% Critical Rate (5% + 25% + 20%). Ignoring Critical Damage bonus from ring, your average damage will be:

> 50% * 85% + 50% * 100% = 92.5%

By boosting your critical rate to 50%, you gain 7.87% (relative to 85.75%) more damage.

What if you use all the critical option available without outside aid? Assuming Br or Bo sub, you already have 50% critical with ring (5% base + 25% stance + 20% ring). Add 14% from 2 Critical Candy Sticks and 20% from 2 Support Rolls. This will add up to 84% critical rate. Your average damage will be:

> 16% * 85% + 84% * 100% = 97.6%

You are almost about 14.82% (relative to 85.75%) more powerful than a non-critical build.

Example, a SuBr uses Deadly Parfait and maxed Critical Strike Tech ring: 84% critical rate and 103% * 110% = 113.3% damage during a critical hit (3% from Ring, 10% from Deadly Parfait). Their average damage will be:

> 16% * 85% + 84% * 113.3% = 108.77%

At more than 80% critical rate, the Deadly Parfait is more powerful than Super Complete Parfait (The damage rate with Super Complete Parfait alone will be 108.13%). And, with outside aid like Shifta Critical and Critical Field, pretty much 100% critical is guaranteed, bumping the overall damage to 113.3%


+++ Simplified Class Damage Calculation
||~ Class ||~ Average Damage ||~ Critical Damage ||~ Critical Rate ||
|| SuBr            || 85% * 105% = 89.25% || 105% * 103% = 108.15% || 39% + 25% + 20% = 84% ||
|| SuFi (P Keeper) || 85% * 103% = 87.55% || 103% * 100% = 103.00% || 39% + 25%  = 64% ||
|| SuFi (Effort)   || 85% * 101% = 85.85% || 101% * 100% = 101.00% || 39% + 25% + 10% = 74% ||
|| SuBo            || 85% * 105% = 89.25% || 105% * 103% = 108.15% || 39% + 25% + 30% + 20% = ~100% ||
|| SuPh            || 85% * 100% = 85.00% || 105% * 103% = 108.15% || 39% + 40% + 20% = 99% ||

||~ Class ||~ Base Damage ||~ Main Stance ||~ Second Stance ||
|| SuBr            || 16% * 89.25% +  84% * 108.15% = 105.13% || 132.98% || 157.16% ||
|| SuFi (P Keeper) || 36% * 87.55% +  64% * 103.00% =  97.44% || 150.05% || 170.52% ||
|| SuFi (Effort)   || 26% * 85.85% +  74% * 101.00% =  97.06% || 149.47% || 169.86% ||
|| SuBo            ||  0% * 89.25% + 100% * 108.15% = 108.15% || 142.76% || 161.68% ||
|| SuPh            ||  1% * 85.00% +  99% * 108.15% = 107.15% || 160.26% || — ||

* This doesn't account other factors such as base attack modification. For more complex calculation, see [[link #sub-damage-calculation | this explanation]]
* Critical rate modifiers are:
 * 39% from base and candies, consisting:
  * 5% from base
  * 14% from 2 Critical Canes (//Critical Candy Sticks//)
  * 20% from 2 Support Rolls
 * 25% from Class Stance Critical, for Br, Bo, and Fi
 * 30% from Critical Field, for Bo (per Balance Patch that buffed Field Remain to 30s)
 * 40% from Critical Stream, for Ph assuming 200 PP
 * 20% from Critical Strike Tech Ring, for Br, Bo, and Ph
 * 10% from Effort Ring, for Fi
* Damage modifiers are:
 * 103% from Critical Strike Tech Ring
 * 103% from Perfect Keeper Ring, for Fi
 * 101% from Effort Symbol Ring, for Fi
 * 105% from Shifta Air Attack Boost, for Bo
 * 105% from Attack Advance, for Br
  * 135% base, calculated based on the contribution of normal attacks to fill PP on a regular SuBr use during EQs
 * 105% from Critical Stream, for Ph
* Stance damage modifiers are:
 * Br: 126.50% Average Stance, 149.50% Weak Stance
 * Fi: 154.00% Brave Stance, 175.00% Wise Stance
 * Bo: 132.00% Elemental Stance, 149.50% Break Stance
 * Ph: 148.50% All Attack Bonus Ph and Photon Stream

++ Skill Rings
If you plan to use more than 1 L Skill Rings, then it is recommended that you use ★12 units. If you play multiple classes, do not put near auto-target on your unit, as it might be wasteful.

++ Technics
Several Technics are essential to have. You can obtain them as drops, buy from player shops, or trade them on Excube Shop at L17 for 20 Excubes each.

||~ Icon ||~ Name ||~ Description ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/tech/tech-li-resta.png| | pso2-icon double pixelate]] || Resta || [[span class="cell-item"]]Heals all allied units in an area.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Pet will request a Resta sympathy if they have a low HP.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Preferred to have Brilliant craft (increases healing).[[/span]] ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/tech/tech-li-anti.png| | pso2-icon double pixelate]] || Anti || [[span class="cell-item"]]Restores status effect of all allied units in an area.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]A full-charged Anti lasts about 5 seconds on the field, you can use this as a preemptive move if you see a status move is incoming, like Elder's ice meteor.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Preferred to have Concentrated craft (reduces charge time).[[/span]] ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/tech/tech-fi-shifta.png| | pso2-icon double pixelate]] || Shifta || [[span class="cell-item"]]Raises base attack power of all allied units in an area.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Pet will request Shifta sympathy during battle.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Pet needs to have the Shifta buff for increased attack; having Shifta buff on the character is irrelevant to pet's damage.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Preferred to have Multi craft (more ticks).[[/span]] ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/tech/tech-ic-deband.png| | pso2-icon double pixelate]] || Deband || [[span class="cell-item"]]Raises base defense power of all allied units in an area.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Deband is not as essential as Shifta, but is a key tech to have for Bo sub.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Preferred to have Multi craft (more ticks).[[/span]] ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/tech/tech-wi-zanverse.png| | pso2-icon double pixelate]] || Zanverse || [[span class="cell-item"]]Inflicts additional 20% Wind damage when the pet is within the Zanverse radius.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Best combined with long-range pets like Popple.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Zanversed pet will affect the DoT given to enemy, as long as the pet is on the Zanverse area.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Preferred to have Concentrated craft (reduces charge time).[[/span]] ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/tech/tech-da-megiverse.png| | pso2-icon double pixelate]] || Megiverse || [[span class="cell-item"]]Restores HP equal to 25% of damages inflicted if the pet is within the Megiverse radius.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Note that since pet does the damage, character will not benefit from the skill at all, unless [[span class="icon op-pot"]]Sinister Stare (Glance of Darkness)[[/span]] is active.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Either craft of Megiverse is fine.[[/span]] ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/tech/tech-fi-safoie.png| | pso2-icon double pixelate]] || Safoie Type-0 || [[span class="cell-item"]]Must have Type-0 Craft (available at start).[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]A movement PA that helps you travel faster. It also tracks enemy's height, and has a lot of hits to help you escape from Fhange (that red-prison thing which traps you in an E-Trial).[[/span]] ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/tech/tech-el-ilzonde.png| | pso2-icon double pixelate]] || Ilzonde || [[span class="cell-item"]]An alternate movement PA if Safoie Type-0 is out of reach.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Has a high PP cost, ditch this once you have Safoie-0.[[/span]] ||





++ Pets
The other important thing beside raising your pet properly is how to use them properly. There are a number of pets for a Summoner, each with their own specific purpose.


+++ Glossary
Please note the following glossary before reading the detailed description of each pets. 
||~ Terms ||~ Description ||
|| DPS Rating || Rating of Damage per second, calculated roughly by damage divided by frame, divided by average damage of Pet PAs. This is solely used to determine which pet attack is more powerful when used over and over. It does not mean that it is the most efficient attack. ||
|| Recall PA || A Photon Arts (PA) that returns pet to your side and stops it from using further attack. This will pause the effect of Near Auto Target ring. Unless noted, pet will perform the move first before returning. ||
|| DoT || Damage-over-time, inflicts small damage over a set amount of time. It is similar to Poison, but is not counted as a status effect, hence do not activate Chase Advance Plus. A DoT skill will sucessfully deliver damage if the first hit lands, even if the part is hidden or the pet got stunned. However, it will cancel if the affected part is destroyed, or pet is unsummoned. ||
|| [[span class="icon op-pot"]]Sinister Stare (Glance of Darkness)[[/span]] || The potential icon on the DPS colum shows the DPS rating when [[span class="icon op-pot"]]Sinister Stare (Glance of Darkness)[[/span]] is used. ||

|content=The DPS value is relative to another pet, use it only to compare one pet to another!]]

+++ Trim & Aero (トリム・アエロ //Torim & Aero//)
||||~ Description ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/pet/pet-torim.png| | pso2-icon quadriple]][[image /img/pso2/pet/pet-aero.png| | pso2-icon quadriple]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]A pet with high powered PAs, focusing on quality over quantity. [[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]They are best suited to pursuit agile enemies with their fast Stab and their double DoT attacks.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Their main weakness is their lack of wide area attack, PP inefficiency, low defense, and weakest normal attacks (i.e. not worth for Spirited Parfait); morover, they are far too reliant on Shouted Spiral to do damage.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]★14 Trim is more durable with higher HP and DEF.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]★14 Aero has higher Pwr, upgraded Aero Spriral (wider attack area) and Aero Slash (stronger).[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Stunned Trim takes 60s to recover; Aero takes 30s.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Trim and Aero are currently outclassed by their glass cannon peers like Synchro, and fellow avian, Rappy.[[/span]] ||

+++ Sally & Popple (サリィ・ポップル //Cery & Popple//)
||||~ Description ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/pet/pet-cery.png| | pso2-icon quadriple]][[image /img/pso2/pet/pet-popple.png| | pso2-icon quadriple]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]A long-range support pet, best at taking down a large quantity of enemies. They are risky to use against bosses that moves too much because its main DPS attack tends to miss. However, their easy-to-use nature allows you to utilize Zanverse together with their attacks.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Sally and Popple can jam: pressing a PA button at the same time when they lose target will cause them to skip the said PA, but will still cost PP![[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]★14 Sally is more durable with higher HP and DEF.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]★14 Popple has higher ATK and upgraded Popple Plode (chargeable and stronger hit, but lower DPS).[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Stunned Sally takes 60s to recover; Popple takes 30s.[[/span]] || 



||~ Personality ||~ Description ||

+++ Marron (マロン //Maron//)
||||~ Description ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/pet/pet-maron.png| | pso2-icon quadriple]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]A defensive pet with self-explosion ability. Marron is best used when your enemy is agressive and have a lot of multi-hit attacks, like Omega Masquerage.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Once was popular pet before damage cap was implemented; with damage cap in place, Marron Strike's capped 1M damage does lower DPS that its required setup.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]] Using Marron for Strike attack only will lower their DPS a lot; consider using the strike as a bonus damage and focus on using Curse and Break as primary DPS.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Marron's size has 3 stages, which powers up their Strike: The 1st stage is default; Marron will always be on 1st stage if you summon them; the 2nd stage is reached after 3 hits, in which Marron becomes big; the 3rd stage is reached after 9 hits, in which Marron becomes even bigger with flames coming out of their body; you have 15 seconds to perform Marron strike before Marron explodes on your hand and reduce your HP to a fraction.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Unlike Melon, Marron has limited use on regular EQs: its overreliance on Marron Strike 3 to do damage become it's main weakness; Marron's main combo (Curse-Break) is weaker than the standard of a Jinga.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]It's niche, however, is in solo "hard difficulty" quest which features multi-hit bosses, i.e. Phaleg, Omega Masquerade, and Shiva; In those quest, you can throw Marron Strike 3 in under 30s and swap to another Marron.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Stunned Marron takes 60s to recover.[[/span]] ||

|| [[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]][[span class="rear-icon flag-na"]]Underdog[[/span]][[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]逆境[[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]Adverse[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]117% damage if HP drops below 25%.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]This is always activated during Marron Strike, thus the preferred personality.[[/span]] ||

|| [[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]キュート[[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]Cute[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]★14 only.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Combines Underdog and Indefatigable, as well as having increased rare item drop rate.[[/span]] ||

||||~ Description ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/pet/pet-melon.png| | pso2-icon quadriple]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]Marron's counterpart, similar PAs with different Strike mechanic.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Melon has lowest HP among all pets, but highest defense among all. Hold the Strike PA button after throwing melon to catch it back.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Melon size's stage is reached faster, needing only 1 hit for 2nd Stage and 2 hits for 3rd Stage.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Melon will revert to 1st Stage after spending 15 seconds on 3rd Stage, instead of blowing up the player.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]A ★14 Melon will have 5000 MEL Def (S-DEF), along with 300 more ATK compared to Marron.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Stunned Melon takes 20s to recover.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Melon have better synergy with Shinkuro's personality than Marron since Melon can do a significantly high damage in a short amount of time, and it's stun timer is shorter.[[/span]] ||

+++ Rappy (ラッピー)
||||~ Description ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/pet/pet-rappy.png| | pso2-icon quadriple]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]An all-rounder pet, very friendly to newbies, although obtaining their eggs is not newbie friendly (Rare drop from ARKS Rappy).[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Rappy invokes the Magikarp Power: its stat before L120 are not impressive, and has 250 points to fill on each stat of Pet Plus; Level 100 and 120 Rappy has attack differences of almost 400 points.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]They notably have strongest normal attacks, albeit smaller hit box; this is both blessing and a curse since it is more accurate to hit a weak point, but easier to miss moving enemies.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]They are the only pet to have a healing PA.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Rappy is the best pet to abuse Braver sub's Attack Advance: Stacking Attack Advance and Spirited Parfait turns Rappy's normal attack into a PA, essentially allowing you to win boss fights by just holding Normal Attack Button.[[/span]] ||

+++ Viola (ヴィオラ)
||||~ Description ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/pet/pet-viola.png| | pso2-icon quadriple]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]A pet that is best for soloing, as it has an effective hate generation, unlike the Launcher PA Fake Silhouette.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Viola's regular PA are similar to Wanda, albeit lacking a reliable area attack.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Viola's stats are not very impressive, and this including their defense.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Using Viola Change, however, increases their defense and changes how their PA works: exerting high-powered damage, high DPS, and enormous hit box in expense of slower animation and sluggish PP regeneration.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Due to how Change mechanic works, Viola is the only pet that gets stronger by using Liberate Takt.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Viola is the signature pet of Manaka.[[/span]] ||

+++ Shinkuro (シンクロウ //Synchro//)
||||~ Description ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/pet/pet-synchro.png| | pso2-icon quadriple]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]The pet for taking down bosses quickly.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Has the highest DPS among all pets, but is hardest to use, and get stunned quickly.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Shinkuro's setup requires you to have superfluous amount of PP (above 150, 185 would be ideal), them to be summoned after a stunned pet (best using Melon); after that, you need to learn to use different PA in order to power up Shinkuro Strike (Synchro Geki).[[/span]] || 




||~ Personality ||~ Description ||
|| [[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]][[span class="rear-icon flag-na"]]Thoughtful[[/span]][[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]なかま思い[[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]Devoted[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]112% damage if Shinkuro is summoned after a stunned pet.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]This is the preferred personality due to its huge boost.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]The boost stays during area change; it will be reset if Synchro is switched out or entering campship.[[/span]] ||
|| [[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]][[span class="rear-icon flag-na"]]Sly[[/span]][[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]こうかつ[[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]Cunning[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]PP Cost reduced by 20% if Shinkuro is summoned after a stunned pet.[[/span]] ||
|| [[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]ジェントル[[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]Gentle[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]★14 only.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Combines Thoughtful and Sly, as well as having increased rare item drop rate.[[/span]] ||

+++ Redran (レドラン)
||||~ Description ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/pet/pet-redran.png| | pso2-icon quadriple]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]A long-range pet that can tear multi-part enemies, such as Ragne and Vibras, quickly.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]On the reverse side, they also can tear down Redran quickly.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Redran has both the highest ATK among all pets, and one of the lowest DEF, with about 120 HP less than Synchro and only 60 DEF better.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Redran, can jam: pressing a PA button at the same time when they lose target will cause them to skip the said PA, but will still cost PP![[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Redran has minimal use on EQ outside seasonal, defense, and endless quests; especially because they tend to run out of PP quickly.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]In addition, Redran is hard-pressed to secure the number of kills it needed in UH fields, further hampering its usage.[[/span]] || 



||~ Personality ||~ Description ||
|| [[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]][[span class="rear-icon flag-na"]]Rambunctious[[/span]][[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]あばれんぼう[[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon pet-persona"]]Rowdy[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]Raises damage by 2% for each enemy killed by Redran; capped at 50% or 150% damage total.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]The bonus will be reset upon entering campship and unsummoning Redran.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]This would be the preferred personality.[[/span]] ||

+++ Vulcan (シンクロウ //Synchro//)
||||~ Description ||

++ Units
Summonners are recommended using [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC[[/span]]-based or PP-based Units. For more information about inits, please see the detailed unit guide, linked below!

++ Harmonizer (タクト //Takt//)
[[image info-2-takt.avif | Takts]]
For most of the time, sticking to your regular harmonizer is fine, but higher rarity harmonizers have additional effects. Harmonizers are handy, for one, they don't benefit a +35 grind as +30 is fine. This applies to even Rykros Staff (it affects Rykros, but hardly significant).

[[div class="pso2-desc-30 pso2-loc-20"]]
+++ Must-have Harmonizers
||~ Icon ||~ Name ||~ Obtain ||~ Description ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/takt/wp-ta-rykrosstaff.png | | pso2-icon double]] || [[span class="cell-item icon wp-ta"]]リュクロス・スタッフ[[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon wp-ta"]]Rykros Staff[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]Zieg Trade[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Deus ESC-A[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]XHEQ Any Omega Boss [[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]][[span class="icon op-pot"]]Sinister Stare (Glance of Darkness)[[/span]] launches a 2000 power projectile attack or 2500 power for Redux (Plus) at every PA during Alter Ego that relies on TEC/T-ATK of the character. [[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]The faster the PA, the more extra damage you can do; it roughly adds ~20% to overall damage during Alter Ego.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Rykros is still the strongest harmonizer available to attack single target, even when you consider Alter Ego only works 66% of time.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Also automatically fulfill Sympathy after 5s (3s if Plus) on Alter Ego: Adds a Shifta tick on Shifta Sympathy and recovers 50% HP on Resta Sympathy.[[/span]] ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/takt/wp-ta-roseskewer.png | | pso2-icon double]] || [[span class="cell-item icon wp-ta"]]ローズスキュア[[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon wp-ta"]]Rose Skewer[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]EQ Christmas[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Collection File[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]The basic all-class harmonizer.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]This is essential to have as it also allows you to use pet when you are raising your subclass.[[/span]] ||
|| [[image /img/pso2/takt/wp-ta-ayer.png | | pso2-icon double]] || [[span class="cell-item icon wp-ta"]]闇征奏アジェルリード[[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon wp-ta"]]Ayer Reed[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]Zieg Trade[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]UH Primordial Darkness[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]][[span class="icon op-pot"]]Cycle of Fate[[/span]] grants 111% damage, -20% PP cost, and persistent 20 PP regen every 10s.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]It's PP recovery will take place anytime, even during charging Technics and using PAs.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Marginally more powerful than Liberate, and a single purchase costs almost nothing.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]You don't ned to make it max 35; a +30 will do no different from +35.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]However, it's only best on Viola and Vulcan, or when you're mobbing with area attack like Jinga Shock.[[/span]] ||

|| [[image /img/pso2/takt/wp-ta-ares.png | | pso2-icon double]] || [[span class="cell-item icon wp-ta"]]アーレスタクト-NT[[/span]][[span class="cell-item icon wp-ta"]]Ares Takt-NT[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]]Zieg Trade[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Tainted Area[[/span]] || [[span class="cell-item"]][[span class="icon op-pot"]]Phantasmal Song[[/span]] grants 103% damage when photon blast gauge is not full and 104% if it is full.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Upon using a Photon Blast, this harmonizer will gain an extra effect based on Photon Blast while the harmonizer is equipped: Helix grants an additional 104% damage; Ajax grants +40% attack PP recovery; Cetus grants +30% natural PP recovery; Julius grants 12% damage resistance Ilios grants -15% PP cost.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Switching to different harmonizer and back will retain the effect; the effect lasts until you return to campship.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Supposedly, if you have multiple mag with different Photon Blasts, you can have all the effect at once if you keep switching mag and do different Photon Blasts.[[/span]][[span class="cell-item"]]Nevertheless, even with just Cetus, this harmonizer can contribute well to PP regeneration, especially if you use a Redran set.[[/span]] ||

[!-- ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== Su FAQ Part ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== ==== --]

+ Frequently Asked Questions (FAQ)
++ What units / armors should I use?
If you just started the game and have no idea on affixing a unit:
* For current North America version, you can obtain Saiki unit set from L61+ Gigur Gunnegam (ギグル・グンネガム //Giguru Gunnegamu//) or Brissa unit set from L66+ Gwahl Jigmolde (グアル・ジグモルデ //Guaru Jigumorude//). Saiki has more PP where Brissa has a bit more ATK and HP. These two sets are the easiest to obtain.
* You can get 1 Weapons Barrier (サブ／ウェポンズバリア) from clearing Cofy (コフィー //Koffie//)'s quest Training Quest Certification (練習クエスト履修証明). You can utilize all 3 characters to obtain multiple copies of it. Until L75, this is the best unit you can obtain.
* You can get 3 Stella Walls (サブ／ステラウォール) from Lachesis the Title Counter when you have 2 L75 classes.
* You can get Ray units — Circu Ray, Circa Ray, Circ Ray — from Badge and Memory Shop. You need 10 Weapons Badge SP on Shop 1 for each of them.

[[#mel-v-tec-mag]]
++  Why not use MEL mag? Doesn't MEL Mag on Fi give 6.5% more damage with all the MEL passives?
In theory where a lot of variables dismissed, yes. However, PSO2's damage factors are actually:
* Stat
* Multipliers
* Attacks or skills used.
* PP circulation, includes max PP, natural PP regeneration, and attack PP regeneration.
* Combo or attack rotation.
* Survival abilities, such as dodge and damage mitigation.
So if you have great stat and multiplier, but lack PP circulation and combo is nothing, you'll have lower DPS.

PP, combo, and survival abilities all depend on the player, so I decided to test this MEL Fi vs TEC Fi myself with the following condition:
* 200 Dex Mag with Megid auto attack, Mag Excite Ring, full mag energy.
 * Dex mag is used because lack of MEL mag.
* Units with total of 180 + 270 ATK.
* Tree Boost and Shifta EX drink is used.
* Jinga A with MEL-SPI Cookie (+120 MEL, +18PP).
* Jinga B with TEC-SPI Cookie (+120 TEC, +18PP).
* Player (Manaka) is a Fem Cast, naturally has higher MEL and lower TEC.
 * Player is a L95/90 SuFi.
 * Player has 284 PP.
 * Player takes all MEL up skills on Fi tree, along with all PP/half/deadline slayers.
 * Player uses Switch Strike when using Jinga A (MEL).
* Player doing 90s of attacking consisting of Jinga Pulsar and Break spam, repeated 4 times (i.e. sample size is 4).
 * Alter Ego is used.
 * Player attacks until PP runs out and let pet normal attacks until PP is full, during normal attack, pet's Shifta is reapplied.
 * All Shifta sympathy requests are fulfilled.
 * At 16s left of Alter Ego cooldown, if Jinga is doing normal attacks, Jinga Pulsar and Break spam will be used for last burst.

The result of average DPS are like this:
||~ Condition ||~ DPS ± St. Dev ||
|| Jinga A + Harmonizer (MEL) || 244K ± 4.8K ||
|| Jinga A + Rykross (MEL) || 322K ± 2.8K ||
|| Jinga B + Harmonizer (TEC) || 235K ± 5.3K ||
|| Jinga B + Rykross (TEC) || 318K ± 6.2K ||
* By comparing average, the MEL and TEC on a regular Harmonizer differs by ~3.8%
 * According to MEDCALC, the significance level is P = 0.0353 (less than 0.05), indicating significant difference.
* On the other hand, the average of MEL and TEC on Rykross is ~1%
 * MEDCALC gives the significance level at P = 0.3584 (more than 0.05), which indicating insignificant difference.

So thus, at same skill level, MEL is only 3.8% better. Further more, when Rykross comes, all MEL-based SuFi will be doing the same damage as a TEC-based SuFi. Considering MEL ability affixes are expensive, using TEC is more reasonable and you can save a lot of Meseta to grab those costumes and lobby actions!

++ What is the best Harmonizer?
This depends on the pet. If we're comparing only Jinga:
||~ Harmonizer       ||~ DPS    ||
|| Guren             || 223K ± 3.4K ||
|| Nemesis           || 221K ± 3.5K ||
|| Raven / Slave     || 220K ± 1.9K ||
|| Rykross Redux     || 273K ± 5.7K ||
|| Ayer              || 249K ± 2.8K ||
|| Rivalate/Liberate || 241K ± 3.2K ||
|| Aqua Ripple       || 226K ± 5.3K ||

+++ Conditions
* Player (Manaka) is a Fem Cast
 * Player is a L95/95 SuPh.
 * Player has 240 PP.
* Mag is 200 Dex Mag with Megid auto attack, Mag Excite Ring, full mag energy.
* Units with total of 240 + 345 ATK.
* Tree Boost and Shifta EX drink is not used.
* Player doing 90s of attacking consisting of Jinga Pulsar and Break spam, repeated 4 times (i.e. sample size is 4).
 * Alter Ego is used.
 * Player attacks until PP runs out and let pet normal attacks until PP is full, during normal attack, pet's Shifta is reapplied.
 * All Shifta sympathy requests are fulfilled.
 * At 16s left of Alter Ego cooldown, if Jinga is doing normal attacks, Jinga Pulsar and Break spam will be used for last burst.

+++ Conclusion
* Jinga is significantly more powerful with a Rykross Redux.
* Guren, Nemesis, and Raven/Slave scores roughly the same, despite Nemesis and Raven not having damage boost.
* Ayer is second-strongest, followed by Rivalate/Liberate

++ What should I use against <Insert Boss Name>?
Wanda or Jinga is always your the first pet that you should use when you are testing new boss or content. They have no real weakness and is suitable for every possible scenario.

++ Wanda or Jinga?
This depends on your playstyle. Wanda has better defense and more efficient Slicer, while Jinga has overall better DPS.

++ Sally (//Cery//) or Popple?
Popple is overall better since it has Charged Popple Plode, a wide area attack; and its main DPS move, Popple Curse, only cost 5 PP.

++ Trim (//Torim//) or Aero?
Trim has slightly better defense, although it doesn't help much. Aero's Charged Shout Spiral 2 have wider area of attack and less PP cost. Since the bulk of Aero's DPS is from Shout Spiral (using 2 seconds Charged Shout Spiral is already better than Shout Slash), having it easier to hit is a big plus. In practice however, both has minimal use.

++ Marron (Maron) or Melon?
Neither are practical for mobbing, so let's assume it's for bossing. Marron is more useful if the boss is overly aggressive and have high attack, like Omega Masquerade, Episode 4 Phaleg, and the inhabitants of the solo XQ Phantom. Melon is more useful if the boss isn't aggressive, and is even more useful of both if you have both a ★14 Melon and a Rykros Staff. Conditions aside, Marron needs to take 9 hits to maximize its Strike PA and it will be incapacitated after. Melon needs only 2 hits to maximize its Strike PA, but if you hold the PA button after the throwing animation, you can catch Melon back, like a yo-yo. Both require you to use their Curse attack once every 10 seconds.

++ What's the viability of Normal Attack Rappy?
In advent of Super Spirited Parfait from Primordial Darkness / Last Boss, Rappy could just use it's accurate Normal Attack without using any PAs. The boost from both Super Spirited Parfaits and Spirited Parfait grants 210% Normal Attacks, this boosts it's Normal Attack score from base 46 to ~96.6. Add extra 111% from Ayer Harmonizer, and you'll get 107.2 score. Add extra 108% from Attack Advance Ring, you'll end with 115.8 score

On the other hand, Rykros Rappy Drop, scored at 95 during 60s Alter Ego and 73 during 30s Alter Ego cooldown, this amounts to the score of [60/90 * 95 + 30/90 * 73] = 87.6. If we apply both Super Deadly and Super Doki Doki, this becomes [87.6 + 1.16 * 1.14] = 115.84 score. Add extra 103% from Critical Strike Ring, you'll end with 119.3 score. However, this score is based on ideal condition where the Rappy has infinite PP.

Since the maximum difference is only [119.3 / 115.8] = 3%, it's safe to say that it's a worthwhile trade.

++ Should I have multiple elemental pets?
It's generally not recommended to, especially if you have limited resources.

++ I'm on PSO2 NA, what parfaits should I use?
If you're new, just use whatever parfait you can get from Pietro's COs, Titles, and Seasonal Urgents. Remember that it's better to focus on putting Pancakes first before parfait!

++ What is the best parfait combination?
I've given my recommendation, but you should use whichever easily available.

++ What is the best parfait combination for Vulcan?

[[div class="table-footnote"]]
|| ^^V^^ || The damage factors in critical rate, critical damage from ring, and critical damage from Vulcan's personality. For example, Super Deadly and Super Doki Doki with 200 PP and Critical Strike Ring yields 49% critical rate. The average non-critical damage is [Non-critical Rate] * [Summoner Average Damage Rate] * [Super Doki-Doki Rate] * [Super Megaton Rate] = 51% * 85% * 114% = 49.4%. The critical damage is [Critical Rate] * [Ring Critical Damage] * [Vulcan's Aggressive Personality] * [Super Doki-Doki Rate] * [Super Megaton Rate] * [Super Deadly Rate] * [Exquisite Deadly Rate] = 49% * 103% * 116% * 114% * 116% * 100% = 77.4%. Add  49.4% + 77.4% = 126.8%. We get the simple damage ratio given by the parfait combination.||

[[/div]]


++ What about <Insert Parfait Name> Parfait?
* Some parfait have niche use, and isn't compliant to the more general use.
* Any parfait that requires you to have certain % of PP is not recommended since your PP fluctuates easily.
* Any parfait that requires pet to have low HP is not recommended since you'll want pets to have full HP.
* Reckless Parfait (がむしゃらパフェ //Daredevil Parfait//) is not recommended because it greatly reduces PP efficiency. It is better for you to PA more often than using normal attacks more often.



++ Do S Eggs have any difference than regular eggs, apart from color?
No. They are akin to having a shiny Pokemon.


++ Why recommend Spirita Cookies at all? Doesn't Shifta affect pet's total Pwr (ATK)?
* This is my personal choice. 15 PP would be more useful in long run, especially with Ph's Critical Stream than just mere ~2% damage.
* The Pwr increase Shifta formula for pet is //(Shifta Power / 1000 + 1% * Number of Support Roll) * (Base Pwr * Drink * Tree + Unit Total Pwr + Pet Plus)//
 * Base Pwr is //Character's Base Pwr + Mag + Status Up Skills + S-Ability Stat Up//
 * Pwr Cookies are not affected by Shifta (analogous to how weapon special ability affix isn't included in Shifta)
 * Pet Pwr are not affected by Shifta
 * Harmonizer/Takts are not affected by Shifta
This is according to [[link https://pso2.swiki.jp/index.php?ダメージ計算 | Damage Calculation on PSO2 Capture Wiki🔗]] and [[link https://pso2.swiki.jp/index.php?ペット#shifta | Pet Shifta on PSO2 Capture Wiki🔗]]


++ What special ability affix do you put in your units?
It changes, surprisingly, yearly. Although 2019 and 2020 was a half-impulse (2019 was on the first appearance of Grace, and surprisingly cheap; 2020 was the first appearance of Grand Spirita, and it was also surprisingly cheap):
* 2017 affix on Qliphad: Tech III, Spirita III, Ares the Soul, Doom Break I, Elegant Tech
* 2018 affix on Evleada: Tech III, Stam III, Ares the Soul, Doom Break I, Grace Tech
* 2019 affix on Lightstream: Ares the Soul, Doom Break II, Persona Reverie, Arks Max, Grace Stamina
* 2020 affix on Orb-Liberate-Nova: S-Ability (S6:Wise Skill, S7:Increased Efficiency, S8:High Minded), Ares The Soul, Doom Break II, Persona Reverie, Fordruss Glare, Grand Spirita
The 2018 Affix has higher [[span class="icon stat-tatk"]]TEC/T-ATK[[/span]] now it's upgraded to DQ Units (and given to other character), but does not affect average damage much.
For more info, please look on the unit guide!

++ Why you seem to focus on PP rather than TEC /T-ATK?
* I like my PP to hit 300 for Maximum Critical Stream bonus :)
* 400 attack affix is a good spot to stop. You can go to 500 for extra 2% damage or 600 for 4% damage, but it's just few percent for a significantly higher budget.
* Your attack affix is only half-relevant to your output. There's also combos, PP management, frame saving, and your enemy's profile.
* Focusing on PP is cheaper and easier. This makes me able to move on to a new set of unit should the need (e.g. future proofing) and impulse (cheap Add Special Ability items) arises, rather than needlessly stick to my old units.
* All my excess meseta can go to sexy costumes and sexy lobby actions.

++ I disagree with your guide!
By all means, contact me on my discord. Your disagreement could be my oversight, and I like to keep updating my guide :)

[[#sub-damage-calculation]]
++ How did you come up with the multiplier number?
The numbers are calculated by comparing total damage from a certain sub to a subless state when using pets.
||~ Sub ||~ Ringed Multiplier^^1^^ (x) ||~ HLB^^2^^ ||~ Class CRate^^3^^ (//a//) ||~ Candy CRate^^4^^ (//b//) ||~ Class CDmg^^5^^ (//c//)  ||~ Σ CRate^^6^^ (//d=a+b+Ring+5%//) ||~ Σ CDmg^^7^^ (//e=c * ring//) ||
|| ― ^^N/A^^ || 1 || 1 || 0 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.39 || 1 || 
|| Fi ^^Brave^^ || 1.5554 || 1.05 || 0.25 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.74 || 1.01 || 
|| Fi ^^Wise^^ || 1.7726 || 1.05 || 0.25 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.74 || 1.01 || 
|| Fo || 1 || 1.05 || 0 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.59 || 1.03 || 
|| Fo ^^PF^^ || 1 || 1.05 || 0 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.59 || 1.03 || 
|| Te ^^EWH^^ || 1.2 || 1.05 || 0.2 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.79 || 1.03 || 
|| Br ^^Average^^ || 1.3314 || 1.05 || 0.25 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.84 || 1.03 || 
|| Br ^^Weak^^ || 1.563 || 1.05 || 0.25 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.84 || 1.03 || 
|| Bo ^^Elem^^ || 1.386 || 1.05 || 0.55 || 0.34 || 1 || 1 || 1.03 || 
|| Bo ^^Break^^ || 1.5593 || 1.05 || 0.55 || 0.34 || 1 || 1 || 1.03 || 
|| Ph ^^200PP^^ || 1.485 || 1.05 || 0.4 || 0.34 || 1.05 || 0.99 || 1.0815 || 
|| Et || 1.3 || 1.05 || 0 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.59 || 1.03 || 
|| Et ^^TACB^^ || 1.495 || 1.05 || 0 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.59 || 1.03 || 
|| Lu || 1.45 || 1.05 || 0 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.59 || 1.03 || 
|| Lu ^^500 Voltage^^ || 1.595 || 1.05 || 0.25 || 0.34 || 1 || 0.84 || 1.03 || 

[[div class="table-footnote mb-2"]]
|| ^^1^^ || Ringed multiplier: Class Damage Multiplier * Ring. This only affects Fi with Effort Ring. ||
|| ^^2^^ || HLB: High Level Bonus ||
|| ^^3^^ || Class CRate: Critical rate from the sub class, assuming all skills taken ||
|| ^^4^^ || Candy CRate: Critical rate from candies,   14% from 2 Critical Candy Sticks and 20% from 2 Support Rolls, totaling 34% ||
|| ^^5^^ || Class CDmg: Critical damage from class. This is usually 1.||
|| ^^6^^ || Σ CRate: Class Critical Rate + Candy Critical Rate + 5%. The base critical rate 5% is added here. For example, with no sub, it is only Candy + 5% = 39% ||
|| ^^7^^ || Σ CDmg: Class Critical Damage * Ring Critical Damage. Ring critical damage is 3% at +20, unless Fi, which uses Effort Symbol ||
|| ^^N/A^^ || This notates subless or "not using any sub class" ||
|| Fi || Fi uses Effort Ring||
|| Br || Br incorporates extra 5% damage from Attack Advance ||
|| Bo || Bo incorporates extra 5% damage from Shifta Air Attack Boost ||
[[/div]]

||~ Sub||~ ARate^^8^^ (//f=1-d//) ||~ AMultiplier^^9^^ (//g=85%*f*x//) ||~ CMultiplier^^10^^ (//h=d*e*x//) ||~ Σ Multiplier^^11^^ (//i=g+h//) ||~ Simulated Damage^^12^^ ||
|| ― ^^N/A^^ || 0.61 || 0.5185 || 0.39 || 0.9085 || 16216 || 
|| Fi ^^Brave^^ || 0.26 || 0.3437 || 1.1625 || 1.5062 || 28230 || 
|| Fi ^^Wise^^ || 0.26 || 0.3917 || 1.3248 || 1.7165 || 32172 || 
|| Fo || 0.41 || 0.3485 || 0.6077 || 0.9562 || 17921 || 
|| Fo ^^PF^^ || 0.41 || 0.3485 || 0.6077 || 0.9562 || 19608 || 
|| Te ^^EWH^^ || 0.21 || 0.2142 || 0.9764 || 1.1906 || 22315 || 
|| Br ^^Average^^ || 0.16 || 0.1811 || 1.1519 || 1.333 || 24983 || 
|| Br ^^Weak^^ || 0.16 || 0.2126 || 1.3523 || 1.5648 || 29328 || 
|| Bo ^^Elem^^ || 0 || 0 || 1.4276 || 1.4276 || 26756 || 
|| Bo ^^Break^^ || 0 || 0 || 1.606 || 1.606 || 30100 || 
|| Ph ^^200PP^^ || 0.01 || 0.0126 || 1.59 || 1.6026 || 30036 || 
|| Et || 0.41 || 0.4531 || 0.79 || 1.2431 || 23298 || 
|| Et ^^TACB^^ || 0.41 || 0.521 || 0.9085 || 1.4295 || 26792 || 
|| Lu || 0.41 || 0.5053 || 0.8812 || 1.3865 || 25986 || 
|| Lu ^^500 Voltage^^ || 0.16 || 0.2169 || 1.38 || 1.5969 || 29930 || 

[[div class="table-footnote mb-2"]]
|| ^^8^^ || ARate: The rate in which you do non-critical damage, or the rate of you doing average damage. This is 100% - Critical Rate. ||
|| ^^9^^ || Average Multiplier: the multiplier of average damage. This is 85% * Average Damage Rate * Ringed Multiplier. Since Su's damage swing is between 70% to 100%, (average of 85%) the constant of 85% is added. ||
|| ^^10^^ || Critical Multiplier: the multiplier of critical damage. This is Σ CRate * Σ CDmg * Ringed Multiplier ||
|| ^^11^^ || Σ Multiplier: Total multiplier of Average and Critical. This is Average Multiplier + Critical Multiplier ||
|| ^^12^^ || Simulated Damage assuming 1000 Base TATK, 400 TATK affix, 2000 Pet TATK, 350 Enemy Defense, and 2000 Power PA. (1000+PF+400+2000*1.6-350) * (0.2*1.05) * 2000/100 * Σ Multiplier * HLB ||
|| ^^Fo^^ || Fo's photon flare is included in the Simulated Damage, notated as PF ||
[[/div]]

After this, all simulated damage is compared to the subless damage. Resulting in a different number than just using the multipliers alone. Please note that the following number is only applocable when using pets.
||~ Sub ||~ Compared Simulated Damage ||
|| ― ^^N/A^^ || 100% ||
|| Fi ^^Brave^^ || 174.09% ||
|| Fi ^^Wise^^ || 198.40% ||
|| Fo || 110.51% ||
|| Fo ^^PF^^ || 120.92% ||
|| Te ^^EWH^^ || 137.61% ||
|| Br ^^Average^^ || 154.06% ||
|| Br ^^Weak^^ || 180.86% ||
|| Bo ^^Elem^^ || 165% ||
|| Bo ^^Break^^ || 185.62% ||
|| Ph ^^200PP^^ || 185.22% ||
|| Et || 143.67% ||
|| Et ^^TACB^^ || 165.22% ||
|| Lu || 160.25% ||
|| Lu ^^500 Voltage^^ || 184.57% ||
